- - The party traveled south from the Silver Marches to Daggerford and noted unusual social silence in the town.
- - The party examined the Tarvis Ulain monument in Daggerford's market square, discovering both the primary commemorative inscription and a secondary Dwarven inscription reading "The move is yours" — matching language from the Castle Ward statue in Waterdeep associated with Stroud.
- - A third inscription on the monument's back confirmed it was witnessed and recorded by Milo Goodbarrel for the Order of the Gauntlet, and funded by the Metalworkers Guild two years after the war.
- - The party entered the River Shining Tavern, where a village priest was selling pewter miniature hand casts of the Tarvis monument for Order of the Gauntlet donations.
- - The village priest confirmed that the Metalworkers Guild possibly had Stroudite members and that the Stroudite inscription was kept because the monument was a gift.
- - Maerith of the Ford approached Grygum in the tavern and reported her daughter Elin, age 7, had been silent for six days, drawing a six-pointed star on every flat surface and speaking in her sleep.
- - Grygum and Daz examined Elin medically and found nothing definitive; Dawnbringer applied lesser restoration.
- - After lesser restoration, Elin spoke one sentence — "The wells are not wells anymore" — then returned to silence.
- - The party concluded Elin's condition was not a conventional disease or fungal infection; she was experiencing audio hallucinations arising from something more profound than lesser restoration could address.
- - A tenuous connection was identified between Elin's hallucinations and Daz's persistent headache, both potentially linked to Underdark madness bleeding upward to the surface.
- - The party moved to the Burning Wizard Inn in Daggerford, where Festrum the gnome innkeeper explained the inn's name: a wizard was burned alive by the Cult of the Dragon at that location decades prior; the south wall soot mark is preserved.
- - A bard performed "The Kenku Could Not Fly" at the Burning Wizard Inn, referencing a Kenku thief called Whistler who took only what was needed from Tiamat's complete hoard.
- - Grygum paid a silver piece for a song about Stroud's chess game; the bard performed a romantic love song in which the chess game served as metaphor.
- - Daz observed a young human scholar at the Burning Wizard Inn reciting a chant with a specific wrongness matching patterns previously observed in Eldred (who went insane) and the Field Ward Preacher; Daz recorded the exact cadence.
- - Zalthir meditated on the inn's roof and returned settled.
- - Thorin spent the evening outside with Dawnbringer, who lit up the surroundings; Thorin found this peaceful.
- - The party traveled the Way of the Lion to Candlekeep.
- - On approach to Candlekeep, Daz's headache worsened, and Grygum identified a possible connection between this and the spreading Underdark madness.
- - At Candlekeep's gate, the party gave books as entry fee; Bookwyrm (First Reader) discovered the party's bag contained both Glabbagool and the miniaturized House T'sarran spy.
- - Kalan Strongbranch (Gate Warden) explained Candlekeep would not take long-term custody of the spy; the party chose to retain her.
- - The House T'sarran spy formally requested imprisonment by Candlekeep guards; this was denied; the decision remained with the party.
- - Glabbagool reported that he and the spy had developed a routine involving her escape attempts, his subduing her, and attempted meditation instruction, and described them as "great friends."
- - Zalthir confirmed to Glabbagool that watching the spy counted as pre-monk training; Glabbagool accepted the assignment.
- - A Candlekeep xenobiologist approached about Glabbagool's monastic training and gave him a vial of Resonant Slime.
- - Zalthir underwent a formal shadow monk trial administered by Khell-Vire and a blind monk; he failed all three skill-based technique assessments (Weightless Shadow, Efficient Strike, Eldritch Claw).
- - Zalthir rolled a 30 to hit the blind monk, who acknowledged having not been hit that hard in a long time.
- - Zalthir destroyed a thrown brick via shadow step in the final challenge; the blind monk concluded "the results do speak for themselves."
- - Khell-Vire assessed that Zalthir's combination of shadow step and the hit redeemed his lack of skills in all other areas, and awarded him a specialized shadow technique; Zalthir chose option C.
- - A note on Khell-Vire's desk reading "Zalthir, the Underdark, until the Bone King sings" was discovered; neither Khell-Vire nor Zalthir could identify its origin.
- - Thorin and Dawnbringer attended a therapy session with Master Archivist Philemon; he assessed Dawnbringer's history and prescribed a two-stage treatment: first remembering daylight without fighting, then grieving her previous wielder.
- - Dawnbringer went silent when asked about her previous wielder.
- - Philemon assessed the bond between Thorin and Dawnbringer as better than he had feared.
- - Daz consulted Sister Yvenne in the Hall of Divination; he disclosed his full history in exchange for information.
- - Sister Yvenne explained Menzoberranzan house structure, the destroy-all-or-forfeit rule, and the story of a house that failed to eliminate one surviving child.
- - Daz reviewed a list of noble house seats; one was marked "struck" rather than eliminated, which Daz identified as anomalous.
- - Sister Yvenne confirmed the public record on one house is sealed and she cannot speak of it in the room; she directed Daz to "other doors."
- - Daz received the heraldry book *Geography and Cities, Menzoberranzan, Pillars of Woe* (approximately 200 years old) and found a footnote referencing "Daz'issin" (lit. "small shadow"); the footnote's appendix had been mechanically razored out at least 50 years prior.
- - Sister Yvenne explained "Daz'issin" as a diminutive mothers used for boys they wanted to live; most houses that used such names did not last.
- - Sister Yvenne deflected when directly asked if she knew Daz's name; Daz read her body language as knowing more than the public record allowed her to say.
- - What it is: A walled town south of the Silver Marches with a market square, walls, cook fires, and horses; far enough south to have missed the Uthgardt barbarian hordes after the giant war but affected by the Cult of the Dragon during the war
- - What happened there: Party passed through; examined the Tarvis Ulain monument; consulted Maerith of the Ford regarding Elin; examined Elin; stayed at the River Shining Tavern and the Burning Wizard Inn; Daz observed the wrongly-chanting scholar
- - Current state: Town carries an unusual social silence — townspeople avoiding certain conversations and topics; monument stands in the market square
- - What it is: A bronze-cast severed forearm mounted on a stone pillar; commemorates Tarvis Ulain, paladin of Bahamut, who died in Daggerford breaking up a Cult of the Dragon recruitment operation three years prior
- - What happened there: Party examined it; discovered both the Stroudite secondary inscription and the Order of the Gauntlet witness inscription
- - Current state: Standing; pewter miniature reproductions sold by the village priest

- - **The Stroudite inscription on the Tarvis monument**: The inscription "The move is yours" appears on both the Daggerford monument and the Castle Ward statue in Waterdeep. Who is coordinating the placement of this inscription, and why? Does the Metalworkers Guild have an organized Stroudite presence? Was the inscription commissioned deliberately, or is there a wider pattern of Stroudite influence on monuments to the war dead?
- - **Daz's identity and the "struck" noble house**: Sister Yvenne appeared to know more than the public record allowed her to say. A noble house record has been sealed at Candlekeep and its appendix physically razored from the heraldry book at least 50 years ago. The name "Daz'issin" (lit. "small shadow") is a diminutive mothers used for boys they wanted to live; most such houses did not survive. Daz is being hunted by

- - Relationships: Positioned as a compromise between and challenge to the Stroudite and Bahamutian schools; distinct from the Draconic school
- - Key members: Grygum (founder), Vareth (first researcher)
- - Recent actions: Producing polemics actively circulating in libraries, taverns, chapter houses, and courts; three duels fought at Candlekeep over the debate

- - Brother Vareth briefed Grygum on the three-school theological debate (Stroudite, Bahamutian, Draconic) consuming Candlekeep and beyond; three duels have been fought at Candlekeep over the debate; courts of two cities have requested written opinions
- - Grygum proposed a fourth school — the strong-weak hypothesis — to Vareth during their meeting in his office
- - Vareth named the school the Grygumite school, coined the term strong-weak hypothesis, and immediately departed to begin his own research
- - Pizwog distributed his syncretic religious analysis paper to Grygum during the meeting with Vareth
- - The party had dinner at the Refectory; prophecies were recited from the gallery above throughout
- - The prophecy *"On the longest night of winter's deepest cold, the keeper falls, the heart is sold"* was recited during dinner; Thorin noted it and retained it
- - A hooded Avowed figure was observed moving past the Refectory at a half-run with something tucked under their robe
- - Glabbagool was confined to the bag of holding for most of the day at Candlekeep; expressed distress upon return to the House of Rest
- - A messenger from the First Reader arrived at the party's rooms before dawn with news of the Keeper of Tomes' death and a summons through the emerald door
- - The party was escorted through the emerald door and to the Chapter House before the Avowed awoke
- - The Council of Twelve convened in emergency session; debate broke out over whether to vote for new leadership, find a successor, or find the murderer
- - Grygum announced himself as founder of the Grygumite School to the council; several members became immediately interested
- - The party was auditioned by the council; Daz summarised their investigative history; the council agreed to delegate the investigation
- - Bookwyrm relieved Kalan Strongbranch of duty in the council chamber before he could speak

- - The party encountered the Candlekeep cat on the tower staircase
- - The party questioned Tadric and established a visitor timeline for the crime scene
- - The party learned that Speak with Dead had already been performed on Janussi's corpse (by or at the direction of Kalan, during his eleven o'clock inspection) and that the dead had not spoken; the rite cannot be repeated for some time
- - The party entered Janussi's chamber and examined the crime scene
- - The party established that Janussi's rib cage was caved in by multiple axe-like blows, his heart removed, and that the blood pattern suggested he may have been dead before his chest was opened
- - The party established that the tobacco smoke and burned-down candles indicated Janussi had been dead for some time before discovery
- - The party established that the safe had been opened, blue gems and empty pouches left behind, and that whatever the thief sought was not the gems
- - The party established that a book (*The Golden Ass*) annotated in fresh midnight blue ink — Janussi's most recent work — was missing from the chamber
- - The party identified that the heart's removal was likely intended to prevent resurrection
- - The party confirmed Tadric's statement that Miss Hollypocket would never have allowed candles to burn to nothing, supporting the conclusion that Janussi was killed well before discovery
- - Thorin requested information about obtaining ice to preserve the corpse for a later Speak with Dead attempt

- - What happened here: The Keeper of Tomes was found dead; an emergency council session was convened; the party was granted investigative authority and carte blanche access

- - What happened here: The emergency council session convened; the investigation was delegated to the party; Kalan was relieved
- - Current state: Session concluded; Bookwyrm, Kalan, and council members have departed

- - What happened here: Janussi was murdered here; discovered by A'lai Aivenmore and Miss Hollypocket; multiple visitors permitted or turned away by Tadric; Kalan conducted a full inspection alone; the party is now investigating
- - Current state: Active crime scene; Tadric on guard; nothing to be removed; Janussi's body present and deteriorating; ice requested
- - What it is: A circular room with lancet windows, a desk, a toppled marble statue and pedestal, a seven-armed bronze candelabra (candles burned to nothing), an open safe, research notes in Celestial and midnight blue and purple ink, and the body of the Keeper of Tomes
- - What happened here: Janussi was murdered; his heart was removed; the safe was opened and gems left behind; a book (*The Golden Ass*) and its contents were taken; Speak with Dead was performed and failed; the party examined the scene
- - Current state: Sealed crime scene; body present; tobacco smoke lingering; candles burned out; missing book; missing heart; spent Speak with Dead rite
- - What it is: A large apartment on the third landing of the Keeper's tower
- - What happened here: Its door was noted as conspicuously ajar during the party's ascent
- - Current state: Uninvestigated; flagged by Daz as requiring examination
- - **Who murdered Janussi?** Cause of death uncertain — he may have been dead before his chest was opened; the heart was removed afterward, suggesting at minimum two deliberate acts
- - **Why was Janussi's heart removed?** Identified as likely prevention of resurrection, but the question of who needed him to stay permanently dead and why remains open
- - **What killed Janussi before the chest was opened?** The blood pattern is inconsistent with the wound; the actual cause of death has not been established
- - **What was taken from the safe?** Blue gems and empty pouches left behind; the thief was looking for something specific that was not jewelry; identity of the item unknown
- - **What is** *The Golden Ass* **and why was it taken?** Janussi's most recently annotated book, in fresh midnight blue ink, is missing; its contents and significance are unknown; it was the focus of his work on the night he died
- - **Who performed Speak with Dead on the corpse, and what did they find?** Attributed to Kalan during his eleven o'clock inspection, but it is unclear whether the rite was performed by him directly or by someone else before him, spending the rite before he arrived
- - **What did Kalan find during his eleven o'clock inspection?** He emerged looking "more determined" than when he entered; Tadric was not watching closely enough to observe his actions inside
- - **What was the nature of Kalan's negligence?** Janussi asked him to resign citing mental faculties and negligence; Bookwyrm declined to specify; the connection to the murder is unexplored
- - **What did Kalan do with whatever he found?** He was relieved before he could report to the council; his findings are unknown and may now be suppressed or acted upon independently
- - **Why did Bookwyrm summon the party specifically, before the Avowed awoke?** She is working a timeline and controlling information flow; her reasons for choosing the party as the instrument of containment are unstated
- - **Where was Bookwyrm at the time of death?** She said she arrived from the baths at nine; did not answer Thorin's direct question about her whereabouts at the time of the murder
- - **What is the significance of the apartment with the ajar door on the third landing?** Noted by Daz as conspicuous; not yet investigated
- - **Who is the hooded Avowed figure seen running past the Refectory?** Moving at a half-run with something concealed under their robe; identity and errand unknown
- - **What was Kazryn Nyantani's relationship with Janussi?** She arrived weeping and distraught; the nature of her connection to the Keeper is unestablished
- - **Why did Fheminor Scrivenbark want to inspect the chamber?** He appeared shocked but requested to inspect — a more active response than expected; his interest in the scene has not been explained
- - **What were the two Great Readers in the Refectory whispering about?** Observed by Thorin at dinner; content unknown; timing (the night before the discovery of the body) may be significant
- - **The prophecy: "On the longest night of winter's deepest cold, the keeper falls, the heart is sold."** Recited during the party's dinner; appears directly relevant to Janussi's death (keeper fallen, heart literally removed); raises the question of whether the murder was prophesied, planned around the prophecy, or coincidental; the phrase "the heart is sold" implies a transaction — who sold it, and to whom?
- - **What happens when Alaundo's prophecy inventory runs out?** Referenced as an open question at Candlekeep; no answer established
- - **What is the significance of the Lolth/Zuggtmoy equivalence document from Asha Vandree's chain mail?** Grygum is tracking this alongside Ilvara's prayer book and the Zuggtmoy marginalia as part of a pattern; the pattern's nature and implications remain unspecified
- - **The Grygumite strong-weak hypothesis: is it correct?** Grygum proposed it by accident; Vareth has accepted it as a serious framework; its relationship to actual events in the campaign (fixed points, constrained choices) remains to be tested
- - **What is Pizwog's research paper actually arguing?** Described as internally inconsistent in meaningful ways; Grygum intends to read it and has flagged the inconsistencies as significant
- - **What is the Candlekeep cat?** Bookwyrm cannot confirm or deny that it is something more than a cat; it stays where it wishes; the question is institutionally unresolved
- - **What unique skill does Zalthir possess that Brother Khell-Vire mentioned to the council?** Referenced but not elaborated upon; the council member asked about it directly

## extract_058.md

### Bullets missing a citation
- - Janussi spent his last morning in one-on-one meetings with Great Readers ahead of Deadwinter Day; half ended in shouting matches.
- - Morning: Kalan Strongbranch passed Hollypocket on the steps with a furious face after meeting Janussi.
- - Around ninth bell: Teles Ahvoste argued with Janussi, shouted "hypocrite," and stormed out.
- - That morning: Sylvira Savikas raged at Janussi, called him a traitor, fired a lance/three darts of force at his armchair (waking Queenie), nearly came to blows, stormed out.
- - Around eleventh bell: Daral Yashenti (hungover) argued with Janussi, called him unfit and a "glorified book peddler."
- - Evening, after supper (~8 p.m.): guests arrived — Fheminor first, then Daral.
- - Half past ten at night: Daral returned drunk, pounded on the door, argued, and fled downstairs — leaving with *The Golden Ass*. Janussi was still alive at this point.
- - Janussi, struck in the face earlier (broken nose), returned to his desk and wrote notes — jokes from *The Golden Ass*, then spell research in *The Discoveries*.
- - Janussi read a poisoned page, ingesting the black substance; tobacco smoke masked the poison's smell.
- - Midnight: bell tower rang; Hollypocket heard a thud (Janussi's death/fall).
- - ~2–3 a.m.: A figure resembling Sylvira (but lacking her scent, carrying an unneeded lantern) descended the tower; Hollypocket glimpsed her glowing red face.
- - The killer rolled the corpse onto its back, smashed open the ribcage, removed the heart, and tore the locket from Janussi's neck. One sapphire and one spell-parchment taken from the safe; a fortune left behind.
- - Dawn: A'lai Aivenmore found the body and knocked over the marble statue in shock.
- - Identity of the killer: who was certain enough to want Janussi silenced on both sides of death (heart removed to block resurrection/speak-with-dead).
- - The black poison remains unidentified — even Glabbagool couldn't name it; described as worth fearing.
- - The poisoned page: was it *The Golden Ass* itself? The missing comedy book carries both the poison and a possible cipher.
- - The gift tag ("read it and weep, you old snob, a proper Dead Winter gift, your loving rival") — points to a rival's poisoned-book gift; ties to Daral and the rivalry theory.
- - Thorin's cipher theory: book + notes as a replacement-cipher key, unresolved.
- - The missing locket: intimate, always worn, torn from Janussi's neck — contents/nature unknown. Possibly held the Hightower Library key.
- - The two Hightower Library keys: Janussi's (missing) and Kalan's — connection to the "deleted" library book/tampering.
- - The broken nose / chipped tooth timeline: nose broken while alive between dinner and death; who struck him?
- - The shapeshifter: a figure wearing Sylvira's form but not her scent, carrying a needless lantern, descended at 2–3 a.m. Was the real Sylvira present, or impersonated?
- - Why three force darts were loosed at an empty armchair (lover's quarrel residue from Sylvira's morning blast).
- - Next steps named: find Hollypocket to confirm what's missing/out of place (done); investigate Daral Yashenti; talk to gate warden Kalan.

- - **Who impersonated Sylvira Savikas?** Walked the halls at night with a lantern; new information to Kalan; the real Sylvira may know who could wear her face
- - **The missing part of a book** that should not be missing — possibly a century old, flagged as suspicious and possibly related
- - **Were the corpse's words ever truly extracted?** Kalan claims questioning the dead failed because the impostor locked its mouth — but this is hearsay and unverifiable
- - **Were the stolen sapphires worth killing for?** Expensive but not rare; tied to the Sapphire summoning spell
- - **The Sapphire summoning spell:** Calls a hidden item to the caster's hand; only works on carried (not merely worn) items; lets one hide the key "nowhere at all" and summon it when needed — but reveals who is holding the item
- - **Will Daral survive midnight?** If he dies, the party concludes he was the innocent poison victim, not the poisoner
- - **Why the rush?** The party is told they can only chase a handful of threads before things move on; Grygum warns "there'll be more murders"
- - **The meaning of Glabbagool's koan answer** — understood by the trained monks but not by Zalthir, who missed the silent-communication classes
- - **The drow in the bag of holding** still requires watching
- - **Kalan's safety:** At risk of being killed over the key; party warned him to beware shapeshifters
